§ 32.01 OFFICIAL NEWSPAPERS.
   The town is designating the “Hanna Update,” “The Rawlins Daily Times,” and
the “Carbon County Comet” newspapers as the official designated newspapers for
the town to publish legal notices, advertise for bids, and other documents as
may be required.
(Res. 2024-595, passed 1-9-2024)
§ 32.02 TOWN CLERK TO BE CONTACT FOR PUBLIC RECORDS.
   The Council authorizes the approval to designate the Town Clerk as the
public records contact, per the Public Records Act, being Wyo. Stat. §§ 16-4-
201 et seq.
(Res. 2023-578, passed 1-20-2023)
§ 32.03 CONFLICTS OF INTEREST.
   (A)   Definition. For the purposes of this section, IMMEDIATE FAMILY means
relatives by blood or by marriage, including wife, husband, children, parents,
grandparents, grandchildren, brothers, sisters, nephews, nieces, aunts, uncles,
brothers-in-law, sisters-in-law, daughters-in-law, sons-in-law, and parents-in-
law, and step relations.
(Prior Code, § 2.32.010)
   (B)   Applicability. Division (C) below does not apply to any qualified
member of the governing body of the town, or any member of that qualified
member’s immediate family, who may receive any monetary or other economic
benefit from any contract made by the governing body if the qualified member
complies with the following. The qualified member:
      (1)   Shall not participate in the consideration or discussion relating
to the contract;
      (2)   Shall not attempt to influence the other members of the governing
body in any way relating to the contract;
      (3)   Shall reveal the nature and extent of any monetary or other
economic benefit he, she, or any member of his or her immediate family may
receive to the other members of the governing body prior to consideration of
the contract;
      (4)   Shall not vote on the matter of granting the contract;
      (5)   Shall absent himself or herself during the consideration,
discussion, and vote on the contract; and
      (6)   Shall not act, directly or indirectly, for the governing body in
inspection, operation, administration, or securing performance under the
contract in which he, she, or any member of his or her immediate family may
receive any monetary or other economical benefit.
(Prior Code, § 2.32.020)
   (C)   Effect on town. No qualified member of the governing body of the town
or any member of that qualified member’s immediate family may receive any
monetary or other economic benefit from any contract to which the town or
anyone for its benefit is a party. The obligation on the part of the town is
void in any contract in which a monetary or other economic benefit will be
received by a qualified member of the governing body or his or her family who
does not comply with division (B) above. Any money paid on the contract may be
recovered by the town from any persons by an action brought in the name of the
town.
(Prior Code, § 2.32.030)
(Ord. 231, passed - -1989)
§ 32.04 DISCLOSURE OF BENEFIT OR INTEREST.
   (A)   Disclosure.
      (1)   No public officer or public servant who invests public funds for
the town or has authority to decide how public funds are invested, shall
transact any personal business with, receive any pecuniary benefit from, or
have any financial interest in any entity other than a governmental entity,
unless he or she has disclosed the benefit or interest in writing to the
governing body of the town. Disclosures shall be made annually in a public
meeting and shall be made part of the record of proceedings.
      (2)   The public officer or public servant shall make the written
disclosure prior to investing any public funds in any entity, other than a
governmental entity, which:
         (a)   Provides any services related to investment of funds by that
same unit of government; or
         (b)   Has a financial interest in any security or other investment
made by that unit of government.
(Prior Code, § 2.33.010)
   (B)   Definitions. For the purpose of this section, the following
definitions apply unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different
meaning.
      GOVERNMENTAL FUNCTION. Includes any activity which a public servant is
legally authorized to undertake on behalf of the government.
      HARM. Loss, disadvantage, or injury.
      PECUNIARY BENEFIT. Benefit in the form of property, but does not include:
         (a)   Property with a value of less than $20;
         (b)   Food or drink or entertainment authorized as a proper deductible
expense of income tax purposes under the United States Internal Revenue Code up
to an amount of $100 per year;
         (c)   Contributions to a political campaign of a public servant as
provided in Wyo. Stat. § 22-25-102; or
         (d)   Benefits in the form of services such as, but not limited to,
transportation and lodging.
      PERSONAL BUSINESS. Any activity that is not a governmental function as
defined above.
      PUBLIC OFFICER. A person who holds an office which is created or granted
authority by the Constitution or the legislature and who exercises a portion of
the sovereign power of the state.
      PUBLIC SERVANT. Any officer or employee of government, including
legislators and judges, and any person participating, as juror, witness,
advisor, consultant, or otherwise, in performing a governmental function.
(Prior Code, § 2.33.020)
(Ord. 265, passed - -1997)
§ 32.05 LAW ENFORCEMENT PENSION PLAN.
   (A)   The town will implement the provisions of IRC § 414(h)(2), being 26
U.S.C. § 414(h)(2), by making employee contributions pursuant to Wyo. Stat. §
9-3-412(b) on behalf of its employees who are members of the State Retirement
System. EMPLOYEE CONTRIBUTIONS shall mean those contributions to the State
Retirement System which are deducted from the salary of employee and are
credited to individual employee’s accounts.
   (B)   The contributions made by the town to the State Retirement System,
although designated as employee contributions, are being paid to the State
Retirement System by the town in lieu of contributions by the employees who are
members of the State Retirement System.
   (C)   Employees shall not have the option of choosing to receive the
contributed amounts directly instead of having them paid by the town to the
State Retirement System.
   (D)   The town shall pay to the State Retirement System the contributions
designated as employee contributions from the same source of funds as used in
paying salary.
   (E)   The amount of contributions designated as employee contributions and
picked up by the town to the State Retirement System on behalf of an employee
shall be the entire contribution required of the employee by the State
Retirement Act, being Wyo. Stat. §§ 9-3-401 et seq.
   (F)   The contributions designated as employee contributions made by the
town to the State Retirement System shall be treated for all purposes, other
than taxation, in the same way that member contributions are treated by the
State Retirement System.
   (G)   This section is effective on the same date that the town first
provided for employee contributions, and shall apply prospectively to all
future employee contributions for the law enforcement pension plan. Employee
contributions shall be picked up and reported as untaxed contributions
beginning with the July 2012 payroll.
(Res. 2012-408, passed 6-21-2012)
§ 32.06 EMPLOYEE PENSION PLAN.
   (A)   (1)   The town will implement the provisions of IRC § 414(h)(2), being
26 U.S.C. § 414(h)(2), by making employee contributions pursuant to Wyo. Stat.
§ 9-3-412(b) on behalf of its employees who are members of the State Retirement
System.
      (2)   EMPLOYEE CONTRIBUTIONS shall mean those contributions to the State
Retirement System which are deducted from the salary of employees and are
credited to individual employee’s accounts.
   (B)   The contributions made by the town to the State Retirement System,
although designated as employee contributions, are being paid to the State
Retirement System by the town in lieu of contributions by the employees who are
members of the State Retirement System.
   (C)   Employees shall not have the option of choosing to receive the
contributed amounts directly instead of having them paid by the town to the
State Retirement System.
   (D)   The town shall pay to the State Retirement System the contributions
designated as employee contributions from the same source of funds as used in
paying salary.
   (E)   The amount of the contributions designated as employee contributions
and picked up by the town to the State Retirement System on behalf of an
employee shall be the entire contribution required of the employee by the State
Retirement Act, being Wyo. Stat. §§ 9-3-401 et seq.
   (F)   The contributions designated as employee contributions made by the
town to the State Retirement System shall be treated for all purposes, other
than taxation, in the same way that member contributions are treated by the
State Retirement System.
   (G)   This resolution is effective on the same date that the town first
provided for employee contributions, and shall apply prospectively to all
future employee contributions for the public employee pension plan. Employee
contributions shall be picked up and reported as untaxed contributions
beginning with the July 2012 payroll.
(Res. 2012-407, passed 6-21-2012)
§ 32.07 CHIEF PAYROLL CLERK.
   (A)   The participation of the political subdivision’s employees in the
state plan shall be governed by the plan document, a copy of which is attached,
the services agreement(s), and any and all rules and regulations promulgated by
the State Retirement System Board.
   (B)   The political subdivision agrees to be bound by and honor the
decisions and action taken by the State Retirement System Board, the Plan
Administrator, in connection with plan management and administration,
including, but not limited to, amending or terminating the plan, selecting
investment options and service providers, and approving unforeseeable emergency
requests.
   (C)   (1)   The political subdivision agrees that no employee shall be
allowed to contribute more than the maximum annual contribution into the plan.
      (2)   If political subdivision offers one or more other 457 plans, the
political subdivision shall coordinate the maximum annual contribution among
all of the plans and agrees that any excess deferrals resulting from
participation in multiple plans shall be attributable to and distributed from
one of the other 457 plans, not the State Retirement System 457 plan.
   (D)   The political subdivision will facilitate educational programs
developed for use with the state plan at least once a year and, to the extent
possible, to allow employees to attend during work hours.
   (E)   The Chief Payroll Clerk is hereby authorized to provide all relevant
information to the State Retirement System 457 Plan Administrator as requested.
(Res. 2011-391, passed 4-12-2011)
§ 32.08 USE OF TOWN VEHICLE.
   (A)   The town purchased a 2008 Chevy Tahoe to be made available to town
employees and town officials to be used to conduct town business, i.e.,
meetings, conventions, conferences, trainings, workshops, pickup supplies, and
the like.
   (B)   When available, this car is to be used for any of the business in
division (A) above. If employees or town officials choose to use their personal
vehicle(s), no mileage reimbursements will be made.
   (C)   If the car is not available for any of the business in division (A)
above and the employee or town official has to take his or her personal
vehicle, then mileage will be reimbursed at the current federal rate.
   (D)   If employees or town officials are attending any of the business in
division (A) above, then meals, lodging, airfare, taxis, and the like will be
reimbursed at the actual cost (receipts provided) less any tips given.
(Res. 2018-485, passed 3-13-2018)
§ 32.09 NOTARY PUBLIC SERVICES.
   (A)   This office adheres to the highest standards of competence and
responsibility in the providing of notary public services.
   (B)   Notaries public in this office shall not perform, nor be instructed to
perform, any notarial act which the notary or the notary’s supervisor knows to
be false or violates notary law or correct procedures.
   (C)   Notaries public in this office shall never notarize any signature
without the maker of the signature first appearing personally before the
notary.
   (D)   Notaries public in this office shall exercise reasonable care to
properly verify the identity of the maker of every signature they notarize by
means of personal knowledge or by valid satisfactory evidence.
   (E)   Notaries public in this office shall correctly maintain a notary
journal of all notarial acts they perform.
   (F)   Notaries public in this office shall refuse to execute a notarial
certificate on any document the truth or legality of which is uncertain.
   (G)   Notaries public in this office shall exercise diligence and utmost
care in the performance of all notarial acts.
(Res. 2013-424, passed 5-14-2013) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 32.10 PROCEDURE FOR EMPLOYMENT OF STATE DESIGN FIRMS.
   The following plan is to be utilized if the town is in need of professional
services for the design of a capital construction project funded by the Office
of State Lands and Investments.
   (A)   If the town is currently engaged with a responsible resident design
firm who is providing satisfactory results, the town may solicit that specific
firm only for a proposal to complete the design work.
   (B)   If the situation described in division (A) above is not met, the town
will provide statewide notice that they are seeking professional services for
the design of a capital construction project. Notice will be provided through
newspapers or other media with statewide circulation at least once each week
for three consecutive weeks.
   (C)   Firms interested in providing the design services are requested to
submit statements of interest in which their project-specific qualifications
and other pertinent information are provided.
   (D)   (1)   The town will screen the statements of interest on the basis of
residency, ability of professional personnel, past performance, willingness to
meet time requirements, location, and current and projected work load.
      (2)   A RESIDENT FIRM has a working office in the state with the
capabilities to complete a substantial percentage of the project work, as
demonstrated by the statements of interest. The work to be conducted by the
firm must be supervised by a professional engineer listed in the state as
required by the provisions of Wyo. Stat. §§ 33-29-301 et seq. and a
professional geologist licensed in the state as required by the provisions of
Wyo. Stat. §§ 33-42-101 through 33-41-117.
   (E)   The goal of the screening process is to select qualified firms to
provide the necessary professional services.
      (1)   If there are five qualified resident firms, those firms will be
selected.
      (2)   If there are less than five qualified resident firms, the screening
process will look to nonresident firms that are proposing to use resident firms
as subconsultants.
      (3)   If there are not five qualified firms identified under divisions
(E)(1) or (E)(2) above, nonresident firms will be considered.
   (F)   The town will provide a request for proposal to the five firms
selected during the screening. The firms will provide separate technical and
price proposals.
   (G)   The town will rank the technical proposals based on project-specific
qualifications, experience, and expertise of the project team, and the content
of the technical proposal.
   (H)   The town will invite three firms for interviews. The firms will be
advised to focus their presentation on price, project-specific qualifications,
and content of the technical proposal.
   (I)   The town will base final selection of the firm on price, project-
specific qualifications, and content of the technical proposal.
   (J)   Please note that residency is not a consideration in divisions (F)
through (H) above. These steps should be fair to all firms that have invested
time and money in the selection process. However, members of the consultant
selection teams are advised that if they are having difficulty deciding between
a resident firm and a nonresident firm, hire the resident firm.
(Res. 2015-445, passed 1-13-2015)
§ 32.11 COVID-19 VACCINE NOT A CONDITION FOR SERVICES OR EMPLOYMENT.
   (A)   The town shall not require any person to present evidence of his or
her COVID-19 immunization status in connection with any service provided by the
town or access to any space or facility operated by the town, nor shall any
employee of the town be required to present evidence of his or her COVID-19
immunization status in connection with his or her employment for the town.
   (B)   The town shall provide full access to public spaces and public
services, regardless of a constituent’s COVID-19 immunization status.
   (C)   The town shall provide full access to goods and services to all
members of the public, regardless of an individual’s COVID-19 immunization
status.
(Res. 2021-541, passed 12-14-2021)
**** ETHICS STANDARDS ****
§ 32.25 DESIGNATION OF PROVISIONS.
   The provisions of this subchapter shall be designated as the “Code of Ethics
for Town Council Members and All Members of Town Boards and Commissions.”
(Prior Code, § 2.34.010)
§ 32.26 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions apply unless
the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   BUSINESS. A corporation, partnership, sole proprietorship, firm,
organization, or other legal entity engaged in buying, selling, or exchanging
commodities or services.
   CONFIDENTIAL INFORMATION. Information which, by law or practice, is not
available to the general public.
   FINANCIAL INTEREST. Any interest which yields, directly or indirectly, a
monetary or other material benefit (other than the duly authorized salary or
compensation for services to the town) to a Town Council member or member of a
town board or commission.
   PERSONAL OR PRIVATE INTEREST. Any interest arising from blood, marital, or
adoptive relationship, whether or not any financial interest is involved, which
is direct and immediate as opposed to speculative and remote and is an interest
that provides the official a greater benefit or a lesser detriment than it does
for a large or substantial group or class of persons who are similarly
situated.
   PUBLIC OFFICIAL or OFFICIAL.
      (1)   Every Town Council member; and
      (2)   Appointed town board or commission member.
(Prior Code, § 2.34.015)
§ 32.27 ESTABLISHED; PURPOSE.
   The proper operation of democratic local government requires that public
officials be independent, impartial, and responsible to the people; that
government decisions and policy be made in the proper channels of the
governmental structure; that public office not be used for personal gain; and
that the public have confidence in and respect for the integrity of its
government. In recognition of these goals, there is established a code of
ethics for all officials, whether elected or appointed, paid or unpaid. The
purpose of this code of ethics is to establish ethical standards of conduct for
all such officials by setting forth those acts or actions that are incompatible
with the best interests of the town and by directing disclosure by such
officials of private financial or other interests in matters affecting the
town. The provisions and purpose of this code of ethics and such rules and
regulations as may be established are declared to be in the best interests of
the town.
(Prior Code, § 2.34.020)
§ 32.28 STANDARDS FOR CONDUCT OF DUTIES.
   (A)   (1)   Public officials are agents of public purpose and hold office
for the benefit of the public. They are bound to uphold the Constitution of the
United States and the Constitution of the state and to carry out impartially
the laws of the nation, state, and municipality and thus to foster respect for
town government.
      (2)   They are bound to observe in their official acts the highest
standards of morality and to discharge faithfully the duties of their office
regardless of personal considerations, recognizing that the public interest
must be their primary concern. Their conduct in both their official and private
affairs should be above reproach.
   (B)   Public officials shall conduct their official duties with integrity,
impartiality, and in the public interest. They shall also conduct both their
official and private affairs so as not to give a reasonable basis for the
impression that any such official can be improperly influenced in the
performance of their official duties. Such officials shall conduct themselves
as to maintain public confidence in their public trust and in the town. They
should not be a source of embarrassment to the town and should avoid the
appearance of both actual and potential conflict between the public duties and
private interests.
   (C)   (1)   No Council member or other official shall, without proper legal
or Council authorization, disclose confidential information concerning any town
official, employee, or any other person, the property or any government affairs
of the town.
      (2)   Nor shall they use such information to advance the financial or
other private interests of themselves or others. This provision does not
pertain to public records, as defined by state law.
   (D)   No Council member or other official, as a result of holding such
office or position, shall accept anything of value or any compensation other
than his or her normal salary. The provisions and definitions of Wyo. Stat. §§
9-13-101 et seq. regarding government ethics relating to the receipt of gifts
by public officials, as they may from time to time be amended, shall apply to
Town Council members and officials, said provisions being incorporated herein
at this point as if fully set forth.
(Prior Code, § 2.34.030)
§ 32.29 WORK AND PERFORMANCE STANDARDS; EXCEEDING AUTHORITY.
   Appointed officials should not exceed their authority or breach the law or
ask others to do so, and they should work in full cooperation with other public
officials and employees unless prohibited from so doing by law or by officially
recognized confidentiality of their work.
(Prior Code, § 2.34.040)
§ 32.30 PREFERENTIAL TREATMENT PROHIBITED; USE OF PUBLIC PROPERTY.
   (A)   (1)   No official shall request or permit the use of publicly
supported property, town- owned vehicles, equipment, materials, labor, or
service for personal convenience or profit, or the private advantage of
themselves or any other person.
      (2)   This rules does not prohibit an official from requesting, using, or
permitting the use of such publicly owned or publicly supported property,
vehicles, equipment, materials, labor, or service which is made available to
the public at large, or by stated public policy for the use of officials in the
conduct of town business.
   (B)   No official shall grant any special consideration, treatment,
advantage, or favor to any citizen beyond that which is available to the public
at large, or which is available to every other citizen.
(Prior Code, § 2.34.050) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 32.31 CONFLICT OF INTEREST.
   (A)   Subject to the disclosure requirements of this section, no Council
member or other official, either on his or her own behalf or on behalf of any
other person, whether paid or unpaid, shall engage in any business or
transaction or shall have any financial or other personal interest, direct or
indirect, which is incompatible with the proper discharge of his or her
official duties in the public interest or would tend to impair his or her
independence of judgment or action in the performance of his or her official
duties. Personal as distinguished from financial interest includes an interest
arising from blood or marriage relationships or close business or political
association.
   (B)   Any Council member or other official who has a substantial or
controlling financial interest in any business entity, transaction, or contract
with the town, or in the sale of real estate, materials, supplies or services
to the town, shall disclose such interest in any matter on which he, she, or
they may be called to act in his, her, or their official capacity. They shall
refrain from voting upon or otherwise participating in the transaction or the
making of such contract or sale.
   (C)   A Council member or other official shall not be deemed interested in
any contract or purchase or sale of land or other thing of value unless such
contract or sale is approved, awarded, entered into, or authorized by him or
her in his or her official capacity.
   (D)   A Council member who has a financial or other private interest in any
action, matter, or legislation pending before Council shall disclose on the
records of the Council or other appropriate authority the nature and extent of
such interest. This provision shall not apply if the Council member
disqualifies himself or herself from voting on any such matter or action, and
recuse himself or herself from the chamber while such matter or action is
pending.
   (E)   Any official who has a financial or other personal interest, direct or
indirect, which is incompatible with the proper discharge of his or her
official duties in the public interest or would tend to impair his or her
independence of judgment or action in the performance of his or her official
duties, and who participates in discussion with or gives an official opinion to
the Council, shall disclose on the records of the Council or other appropriate
authority the nature and extent of such interest.
   (F)   Specific conflicts of interest are enumerated below for the guidance
of officials.
      (1)   No Council member or official shall engage in private employment
when such employment is incompatible with, or gives the appearance of
incompatibility with, the proper discharge of his, her, or their official
duties, or would tend to impair his, her, or their independence of judgment or
action in the performance of his, her, or their official duties.
      (2)   (a)   No Council member or other official shall appear on behalf of
private interests before any board or commission of the town or Town Council. A
Council member or official shall not represent private interests in any action
or proceeding against the interests of the town in any litigation to which the
town is a part.
         (b)   A Council member may appear before a town board or commission on
behalf of constituents in the course of their duties as a representative of the
electorate or in the performance of public or civic obligations. However, no
Council member or other official shall accept a retainer or compensation that
is contingent upon a specific action by a town board or commission.
(Prior Code, § 2.34.070) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 32.32 POLITICAL ACTIVITY.
   (A)   No town official shall use the prestige of his or her position on
behalf of any political party.
   (B)   A town official shall not use public funds, time, personnel,
facilities, or equipment for political or campaign activity unless the use is:
      (1)   Authorized by law; or
      (2)   Properly incidental to another activity required or authorized by
law and the public official, allocates or reimburses the town for any
additional costs incurred for that portion of the activity not required or
authorized by law.
   (C)   No town official, whether elected or appointed, shall promise an
appointment to any municipal position as a reward for any political activity.
(Prior Code, § 2.34.080)
§ 32.33 APPLICABILITY; ADVISORY OPINIONS.
   When a Council member or other official has doubt as to the applicability of
a provision of this code of ethics to a particular situation, they shall apply
to the Town Attorney for an advisory opinion and be guided by that opinion when
given. The Council member or other official shall have the opportunity to
present their interpretation of the facts at issue and of the applicable
provision of this code of ethics before such advisory decision is made. This
code of ethics shall be operative in all instances covered by its provisions
except when superseded by an applicable statutory, town code provision or
policy, and statutory or town code action is mandatory, or when the application
of a statutory or town code provision is discretionary, but determined to be
more appropriate or desirable.
(Prior Code, § 2.34.090)
§ 32.34 ENFORCEMENT; VIOLATION.
   (A)   Failure to comply with this subchapter constitutes improper conduct.
   (B)   Violation of any provisions of this code of ethics should raise
conscientious questions for the Council member or other official concerned as
to whether voluntary resignation or other action is indicated to promote the
best interests of the town. Violation by any appointed official or Council
member may constitute a reason for removal from office.
   (C)   No Council member or any member of that Council member’s immediate
family, the town, or anyone for its benefit is a party. The obligation on the
part of the town is void in any contract in which a monetary or other economic
benefit will be received by a Council member or his or her family who does not
comply with §
32.31. Any money paid on the contract may be recovered by the town from any
persons by an action brought in the name of the town.
(Prior Code, § 2.34.100) (Ord. 326, passed 8-11-2009) Penalty, see §
10.99

**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 33.01 HAZARD MITIGATION PLAN ADOPTED.
   The State Region 3 Hazard Mitigation Plan, 2025 Update, is hereby adopted by
reference as this jurisdiction’s multi-hazard mitigation plan.
(Res. 2025-616, passed 5-13-2025)
**** EMERGENCY MANAGEMENT ORGANIZATION ****
§ 33.15 CREATION.
   (A)   There is hereby created the Town Emergency Management Organization,
which shall be governed by a board consisting of:
      (1)   The Mayor and Town Council members;
      (2)   The Chairperson of the Board shall be the Mayor; and
      (3)   The Vice-Chairperson of the Board shall be the Fire Chief.
   (B)   The Board will become an integral part of the County Emergency
Management Organization during a countywide disaster.
(Prior Code, § 2.10.010) (Ord. 181-A, passed - -1984; Ord. 230, passed - -1989)
§ 33.16 CHIEF ELECTED OFFICIAL; POWERS AND DUTIES.
   The primary responsibility for initiating action at the time of an emergency
or disaster rests with the elected government of the town. A declaration of an
emergency and subsequent assistance requests during or on account of an
emergency or disaster in the town will be honored only when the emergency is
declared by the chief elected official and/or Town Council.
(Prior Code, § 2.10.020) (Ord. 181-A, passed - -1984)
§ 33.17 EMERGENCY OPERATION PLAN.
   The Town Emergency Board is empowered to prepare, review, and recommend to
the County Emergency Management Organization an emergency operation plan for
the city.
(Prior Code, § 2.10.030) (Ord. 181-A, passed - -1984)
§ 33.18 RULES AND REGULATIONS.
   It shall be the duty of the Board to promulgate such rules and regulations
necessary for the efficient management and operation of the Town Emergency
Management Organization.
(Prior Code, § 2.10.040) (Ord. 181-A, passed - -1984)
§ 33.19 EMERGENCY OPERATING EQUIPMENT.
   The Town Emergency Board shall designate a suitable location of the
establishment for emergency operating equipment and, if practicable, fallout
protection.
(Prior Code, § 2.10.050) (Ord. 181-A, passed - -1984)
§ 33.20 LOCATION FOR EMERGENCY OPERATIONS CENTER.
   The emergency operations center for the town shall be located at the Town
Police Department Dispatch Center. Emergency situations may warrant the
establishment of a point of operation at the scene of the disaster.
(Prior Code, § 2.10.060) (Ord. 181-A, passed - -1984)
§ 33.21 SUCCESSION OF AUTHORITY.
   The succession of authority within the Town Emergency Board will be:
   (A)   Mayor;
   (B)   Mayor Pro Tem; and
   (C)   One Council member.
(Prior Code, § 2.10.070) (Ord. 181-A, passed - -1984)
§ 33.22 COORDINATOR; RECOMMENDATION.
   The Town Emergency Board shall recommend to the Governor of the state a
person to serve as coordinator for the Board, who shall serve at the pleasure
of the Board and the Governor.
(Prior Code, § 2.10.080) (Ord. 181-A, passed - -1984)
§ 33.23 COORDINATOR; POWERS AND DUTIES.
   The Coordinator is empowered and directed:
   (A)   To prepare a local emergency operating plan for the town and assist in
the development of a countywide emergency operations plan;
   (B)   To assist in the coordination of emergency responsibilities and
functions of other departments, industries, and citizens of the town;
   (C)   To execute directives and policies established by the Town Emergency
Board; and
   (D)   To coordinate training, planning, management, and operational
activities with the County Emergency Management Department.
(Prior Code, § 2.10.090) (Ord. 181-A, passed - -1984)
§ 33.24 DESIGNATION OF AGENTS.
   The Town Emergency Board will, with the consent of the Governor, designate
agent(s) to receive services, equipment, funds, and the like offered by
federal/state grant or loan and other donors.
(Prior Code, § 2.10.100) (Ord. 181-A, passed - -1984)

   The Town Clerk is authorized and directed to collect bonds for and on behalf
of the Town Police Judge in such amounts as from time to time the Town Police
Judge shall determine to be appropriate. Proper records of all sums paid shall
be kept by the Town Clerk, and the Town Clerk shall have authority to issue
receipts for such amounts. All amounts so collected shall be handled in the
same manner as other bond forfeitures are handled and a disposition made in the
same manner.
(Prior Code, § 3.04.010) (Ord. 49, passed - -1976)
§ 34.02 ANNUAL BUDGET.
   (A)   Wyo. Stat. §15-2-201(a) and (d) is made inapplicable to the town.
(Prior Code, § 3.12.010)
   (B)   At the request of the Mayor, or upon its own motion, after five days’
publication of notice, the Town Council may, by resolution, amend its annual
budget at any time after its adoption:
      (1)   To include anticipated or unanticipated revenues or expenditures;
      (2)   To correct errors and omissions;
      (3)   To transfer any unencumbered or unexpended appropriation from one
fund, department, or account to another;
      (4)   To increase or decrease departmental expenditures;
      (5)   To increase or decrease the appropriation of any fund; or
      (6)   Any other matters which may ensure fiscal responsibility.
(Prior Code, § 3.12.020)
   (C)   The fiscal year for the town shall commence on July 1 of each calendar
year and continue through June 30 of the succeeding year.
(Prior Code, § 3.12.030)
(Ord. 176, passed - -1983)
**** INVESTMENT POLICY ****
§ 34.15 APPLICATION.
   This investment policy applies to all financial assets of the town. These
funds are accounted for in the town’s monthly financial statement and include
the following funds and any additional funds procured throughout the fiscal
year:
   (A)   General Fund;
   (B)   Enterprise Funds:
      (1)   Water; and
      (2)   Sewer.
   (C)   Special funds.
(Res. 2016-455, passed 8-8-2016)
§ 34.16 POLICY.
   It is the policy of the town to invest public funds in a manner which will
provide a reasonable rate investment return with maximum security, while
meeting the anticipated cash flow demands of the town and conforming to all
federal, state, and local laws and regulations governing the investment of
public funds.
(Res. 2016-455, passed 8-8-2016)
§ 34.17 MANAGEMENT RESPONSIBILITY.
   Management responsibility for the investment program is hereby delegated to
the Town Treasurer, who may establish written procedures for the operation of
the investment program consistent with this investment policy. The Town
Treasurer shall be responsible for all transactions undertaken and shall
establish a system of controls to regulate investment activities. The Town
Treasurer is authorized to make such investments as will conform to this policy
which shall be deemed at least as restrictive as the types of investments
authorized by Wyo. Stat. § 9-4-831(a). No person may engage in an investment
transaction except as permitted by this policy and any procedures established
by the Town Treasurer.
(Res. 2016-455, passed 8-8-2016)
§ 34.18 JUDGMENT AND CARE.
   Investments shall be made with judgment and care, under circumstances then
prevailing, which persons of prudence, discretion, and intelligence exercise in
the management of their affairs, not for speculation, but for investment,
considering the probable safety of their capital as well as the probable income
to be derived.
(Res. 2016-455, passed 8-8-2016)
§ 34.19 STANDARDS OF PRUDENCE.
   The standard of prudence to be used by the investment officials shall be the
prudent person standard and shall be applied in the context of managing an
overall portfolio. Investment officers acting in accordance with written
procedures and the investment policy and exercising due diligence shall be
relieved of personal responsibility for and individual security’s risk or
market price changes, provided deviations from expectations are reported in a
timely fashion and appropriate action is taken to control adverse developments.
(Res. 2016-455, passed 8-8-2016)
§ 34.20 PRIMARY OBJECTIVES.
   (A)   The primary objectives, in priority order, of the town investment
activity shall be:
      (1)   Safety;
      (2)   Liquidity;
      (3)   Return on investment; and
      (4)   Community investment.
   (B)   The town shall only invest in securities allowed under Wyo. Stat. § 9-
4-831, with no securities maturing more than five years from the date of
settlement.
(Res. 2016-455, passed 8-8-2016)
§ 34.21 COLLATERIZATION.
   (A)   In addition to the collaterization requirements found in Wyo. Stat. §
9-4-821, collaterization will also be required on investments with the
financial institutions when public moneys on deposit exceed the amount insured
by the Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation. The collaterization level will be
110%, or higher as required by law, of the market value of principal and
accrued interest.
   (B)   The collateral pledge shall always be held by an independent third
party with whom the town has a current custodial agreement, as required by
state statutes.
(Res. 2016-455, passed 8-8-2016)
§ 34.22 SIGNED STATEMENT.
   Any person effecting an investment transaction on behalf of the town or
offering investment advice to the town’s governing body shall sign a statement
indicating he or she has read this policy and agrees to abide by this policy
and by applicable federal, state, and local laws with respect to the
transactions undertaken and investment advice given.
(Res. 2016-455, passed 8-8-2016)
**** LOW-RENT HOUSING PROJECTS ****
§ 34.35 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions apply unless
the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   PROJECT. Any low-rent housing hereafter developed as an entity by the town
with financial assistance of the United States of America, acting through the
Secretary of Housing and Urban Development (hereinafter called the
“government”), excluding, however, any low-rent housing project covered by any
contract for loans and annual contributions entered into between the town and
the government, or its predecessor agencies, prior to the date of the ordinance
codified in this subchapter.
   SHELTER RENT. The total of all charges to all tenants of a project for
dwelling rents and nondwelling units (excluding all other income of such
project), less the cost to the town of all dwelling and nondwelling utilities.
   SLUM. Any area where dwellings predominate which, by reason of dilapidation,
overcrowding, faulty arrangement, or design, lack of ventilation, light, or
sanitation facilities, or any combination of these factors, are detrimental to
safety, health, or morals.
   TAXING BODY. The state or any political subdivision or taxing unit thereof
in which a project is situated and which would have authority to assess or levy
real or personal property taxes or to certify such taxes to a taxing body or
public officer to be levied for its use and benefit with respect to a project
if it were not exempt from taxation.
(Prior Code, § 3.08.010) (Ord. 100, passed - -1980)
§ 34.36 OBJECTIVES.
   The town shall deliver:
   (A)   To secure a contract or contracts with the government for loans and
annual contributions covering a project comprising 20 units of low-rent
housing; and
   (B)   To develop and administer such projects which shall be located within
the corporate limits of the town. The obligations of the town hereto shall
apply to such projects.
(Prior Code, § 3.08.020) (Ord. 100, passed - -1980; Ord. 159, passed - -1982)
§ 34.37 PAYMENTS IN LIEU OF TAXES.
   (A)   Under the Constitution and statutes of the state, all projects are
exempt from all real and personal property taxes and special assessments levied
or imposed by any taxing body. With respect to any project, so long as either:
      (1)   Such project is owned by a public body or governmental agency and
is used for low-rent housing purposes;
      (2)   Any contract between the town and the government for loans or
annual contributions, or both, in connection with such project remains in force
and effect; or
      (3)   Any bonds issued in connection with such project or any moneys due
to the government in connection with such project remain unpaid; whichever
period is the longest, the town declares that it will not levy or impose any
real or personal property taxes for special assessments upon such project or
upon the town with respect thereto. During such period, the town shall make
annual payments (herein called “payments in lieu of taxes”) in lieu of all
property taxes and special assessments and in payment for the public services
and facilities furnished from time to time without other cost or charge for or
with respect to such project.
   (B)   Each such annual payment in lieu of taxes shall be made after the end
of the fiscal year established for such project, and shall be in an amount
equal to either:
      (1)   Ten percent of the shelter rent charged by the town in respect to
such project during such fiscal year; or
      (2)   The amount permitted to be paid by applicable state law in effect
on the date such payment is made, whichever amount is the lower.
   (C)   The town shall distribute the payments in lieu of taxes among the
taxing bodies in the proportion which the real property taxes which would have
been paid to each taxing body for such year if the project were not exempt from
taxation bear to the total real property taxes which would have been paid to
all of the taxing bodies for such year if the project were not exempt from
taxation; provided, however, that no payment for any year shall be made to any
taxing body in excess of the amount of the real property taxes which would have
been paid to such taxing body for such year if the project were not except from
taxation.
   (D)   Upon failure of the town to make any payment in lieu of taxes, no lien
against any project or assets of the town shall attach, nor shall any interest
or penalties accrue or attach, on account thereof.
(Prior Code, § 3.08.030) (Ord. 100, passed - -1980)
§ 34.38 DEVELOPMENT AND ADMINISTRATION.
   During the period commencing with the date of the acquisition of any part of
the site or sites of the project and continuing so long as either:
   (A)   Such project is owned by a public body or governmental agency and is
used for low rent housing purposes;
   (B)   Any contract between the town and the government for loans or annual
contributions, or both, in connection with such projects remains in full force
and effect; or
   (C)   Any bonds issued in connection with such project remain unpaid;
whichever period is the longest, the town without cost or charge to the tenants
of such project (other than the payments in lieu of taxes) shall:
      (1)   Furnish or cause to be furnished to the tenants of such project
public services and facilities of the same character and to the same extent as
are furnished from time to time without cost or charge to other dwellings and
inhabitants in the town;
      (2)   Vacate such streets, roads, and alleys within the area of such
project as may be necessary in the development thereof; and, insofar as it is
lawfully able to do so without cost or expense to the town, cause to be removed
from such vacated areas, insofar as it may be necessary, all public or private
utility lines and equipment;
      (3)   Insofar as the town may lawfully do so:
         (a)   Grant such deviations from the Building Code of the town as are
reasonable and necessary to promote economy and efficiency in the development
and administration of such project, and at the same time safeguard health and
safety; and
         (b)   Make such changes in any zoning of the site and surrounding
territory of such project as are reasonable and necessary for the development
and protection of such project and the surrounding territory.
      (4)   Accept grants of easements necessary for the development of such
project; and
      (5)   Take such other lawful actions or act in such ways as the town may
find necessary in connection with the development and administration of such
project.
(Prior Code, § 3.08.040) (Ord. 100, passed - -1980)
§ 34.39 IMPROVEMENTS.
   In respect to any project, the town further agrees that within a reasonable
time after realization of a need to do so, it will:
   (A)   Dedicate for public use all interior streets, roads, alleys, and
adjacent sidewalks within the area of such project, together with all storm and
sanitary sewer mains in such dedicated areas, after the town at its own
expense, for which it will reimburse itself from project funds, but only to the
extent such funds are available for such reimbursement, has completed the
grading, improvement, paving, and installation thereof in accordance with town
specifications;
   (B)   Dedicate for public use land for, and will grade, improve, pave, and
provide sidewalks for all streets bounding such project or necessary to provide
adequate access thereto, and will reimburse itself from project funds, but only
to the extent such funds are available for such reimbursement, such amount as
would be assessed against the project site for such work if such site were
privately owned; and
   (C)   Provide, or cause to be provided, water mains, and storm and sanitary
sewer mains, leading to such project and serving the bounding streets thereof,
and will reimburse itself from projects funds, but only to the extent such
funds are available for such reimbursement, such amounts as would be assessed
against the project for such work if such site were privately owned.
(Prior Code, § 3.08.050) (Ord. 100, passed - -1980)
§ 34.40 FAILURE OF TOWN TO FURNISH PUBLIC SERVICES AND FACILITIES.
   If, by reason of the town’s failure or refusal to furnish or cause to be
furnished any public services or facilities which it has declared in this
subchapter it intends to furnish or to cause to be furnished to the project or
to the tenants of any project, funds for such project are used to pay for any
expense to obtain such services or facilities, then an amount equal to such
expense shall be deducted from any payments in lieu of taxes due or to become
due to the town in respect to such project or any other low rent housing
projects owned or operated by the town.
(Prior Code, § 3.08.060) (Ord. 100, passed - -1980)
§ 34.41 COOPERATION ORDINANCES.
   No cooperation ordinance heretofore adopted by the town shall be construed
to apply to the projects covered by this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 3.08.070) (Ord. 100, passed - -1980)
§ 34.42 OBLIGATION.
   So long as any contract between the town and the government for loans
(including preliminary loans) or annual contributions, or both, in connection
with the project remains in force and effect, or so long as any bonds issued in
connection with the project or any moneys due to the government in connection
with the project remain unpaid, this subchapter shall not be abrogated,
changed, or modified without the consent of the government. The privileges and
obligations of the town under this subchapter shall remain in full force and
effect with respect to the project so long as the beneficial title to such
project is held by the town or by any other public body or governmental agency,
including the government, authorized by law to engage in the development or
administration of low-rent housing projects. If at any time the beneficial
title to, or possession of, the project is held by such other public body or
governmental agency, including the government, the provisions of this
subchapter shall inure to the benefit of and may be enforced by such other
public body or governmental agency, including the government.
(Prior Code, § 3.08.080) (Ord. 100, passed - -1980)

CHAPTER 50: UTILITIES GENERALLY

Utility Non-User Agreements

**** UTILITY NON-USER AGREEMENTS ****
§ 50.01 REDUCED CHARGES FOR NON-USERS.
   (A)   The owner of property which uses utility services within the town,
which property is not and will not be used as a residence or a business and
which will remain completely vacant and unoccupied for a period of time of not
less than three consecutive months and not to exceed 12 consecutive months,
after appropriately and completely executing and delivering a non-user
agreement as provided in §
50.02, may have utility charges to the property reduced to the following:
      (1)   Sewer: $1 per month; and
      (2)   Landfill: $21 per month.
   (B)   The Town Clerk, Assistant Town Clerk, or Mayor are authorized to
execute this agreement on behalf of the town without further Council approval.
(Prior Code, § 13.12.010) (Ord. 351, passed 4-8-2012)
§ 50.02 NON-USER AGREEMENT.
   Before the reduced charges for non-use of utilities can be extended to a
property, the owner of property which uses utility services shall appropriately
and completely execute and deliver to the Town Clerk’s office, and agree to be
bound by the terms of, a non-user agreement in substantially the following
form:
NON-USER AGREEMENT
THIS NON-USER AGREEMENT is between the Town of Hanna and (“Owner”)
THE PARTIES AGREE AS FOLLOWS:
1.                                                                    (“Owner”) is the owner of property within the Town of Hanna at street address: .
   Owner requests the Town of Hanna place the property on non-user status on the terms and conditions stated on this agreement.
 
2.   Owner warrants and represents and promises to the Town of Hanna that the property is not and will not be used as a residence or a business and will remain completely vacant and unoccupied during the time period beginning                            , 20       , and
continuing until                               , 20     , at which time this agreement expires by its own terms. This agreement be extended one time by a written notice from Owner to Town so long as the extension is received by the Town prior to the expiration date of this
non-user agreement and so long as the total period of non-use does not exceed 12 consecutive months.
3.   The Owner understands the minimum term of a non-user agreement is three consecutive months and that the term cannot exceed 12 consecutive months.
4.   Owner acknowledges and agrees the Town shall make periodic inspections of the property to ascertain whether it is completely vacant and unoccupied during the period stated in paragraph 2. In the event the property is occupied, Owner agrees to pay to the Town the minimum
rate in effect for water, sewer and landfill service commencing with the beginning date stated in paragraph 2 and this agreement will immediately be of no force or effect. In addition, Owner acknowledges that the maximum penalty for violation of Section 8.04.110 of the code
pertaining to landfill §
52.99 of the code pertaining to sewers is the sum of not more than $750 for each violation. Owner further acknowledges that residing in or occupying the property, leasing the property, allowing someone else to use, occupy, or reside on the property or any other occupation or
use of the property at any time during the period of non-use stated in Paragraph 1 constitutes a violation of the Hanna Municipal Code for which Owner may be prosecuted.
5.   Owner wishes to have the sewer and landfill fees decreased for Owner’s property for the following reasons:
                                                                                                                                            
                                                                                                                                            
                                                                                                                                            
6.   Owner can be contacted at the following address and telephone number during the term of this agreement:
                                                                                                                                            
                                                                                                                                            
7.   Owner agrees to immediately notify Town of any change in address or phone number.
8.   Owner understands that, as of the end of this agreement, full and regular charges shall resume on the property unless:
   1) A timely extension is received prior to the expiration date of this agreement as allowed in paragraph 2.; or
   2) A new agreement is signed with the Town of Hanna.
9.   This agreement supersedes all prior non-use agreements and becomes effective on the beginning date stated in paragraph 2.
 
(Prior Code, § 13.12.020) (Ord. 289, passed - -2001)
§ 50.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person who violates any provisions of this chapter and
particularly who violates, during the term thereof, any of the terms and
conditions of a non-user agreement which has been delivered to the town, shall,
upon conviction, be guilty of a misdemeanor, and each day of violation shall
constitute a separate offense.
(Prior Code, § 13.12.030) (Ord. 211, passed - -1987)
CHAPTER 51: WATER
Section

**** WATER SYSTEM ****
§ 51.01 OWNERSHIP AND CONTROL.
   The system of waterworks distribution and supply of the town, located within
and without the corporate limits of the town, are owned by the town and
maintained, controlled, and managed exclusively by the Town Council for
municipal purposes, the object being to supply the inhabitants thereof with
water for domestic, manufacturing, irrigation and other purposes within the
town limits. The Town Council controls and approves all additions, changes, and
deletions to the system and all connections and disconnections to the
waterworks systems.
(Prior Code, § 13.04.010)
§ 51.02 MANAGEMENT OF WATERWORKS SYSTEM.
   The Town Council controls, manages, and maintains the waterworks system as
set forth in §
51.01. In the furnishing and maintenance of the water services provided in this
chapter, the Town Council may be represented, when it deems necessary, by the
Public Works Director, who is a department head of the town. The duties of the
Public Works Director, as they relate to the waterworks system, include, but
are not limited to, superintending the installation and maintenance of the
water distribution system; giving reports of his or her activities to the Town
Council at a regular time designated by them or upon request; auditing bills or
claims for work done or material furnished to the system of waterworks of the
town prior to payment by the Town Council; and such other duties as may be
assigned from time to time.
(Prior Code, § 13.04.020)
§ 51.03 RATES AND PAYMENT FOR SERVICE.
   (A)   The Town Council has the authority to establish, by resolution from
time to time, rates for water consumption, costs, and services incidental for
the operation of the waterworks system.
   (B)   Rates for residential users:
Water Usage (Gallons) Water Rate (per 1,000 Gallons)
Water Usage (Gallons) Water Rate (per 1,000 Gallons)
0 to 7,000            $47.10 (base rate)
7,001 to 14,000       $1.49
14,001 to 21,000      $1.76
21,001 to 28,000      $1.96
28,001 to 35,000      $2.29
35,001 to 42,000 plus $2.62
Notes to Table:
   - Minimum charge of $47.10 per month
 
   (C)   Commercial, industrial, and school users:
Water Usage (Gallons) Water Rate (per 1,000 Gallons)
Water Usage (Gallons) Water Rate (per 1,000 Gallons)
0 to 7,000            $47.10 (base rate)
7,001 to 14,000       $1.49
14,001 to 21,000      $1.76
21,001 to 28,000      $1.96
28,001 to 35,000      $2.29
35,001 to 42,000 plus $2.62
Notes to Table:
   - Minimum charge of $47.10 per month
 
   (D)   Trailer courts and multiple units, when attached to the same tap, will
be billed to the property owner based upon the total number of gallons consumed
as measured by a master meter installed on the main line. Trailer courts and
multiple units will also be billed $10 per month per active unit as a
maintenance fee.
   (E)   Bulk water rate from the town:
      (1)   Forty dollars for the first 10,000 gallons less of water from the
wells designated by the town, and $4 for every 1,000 gallons thereafter;
      (2)   For processed water, there will be a $500 refundable deposit
required, along with the following fees. For processed water, the charge will
be $10 per 1,000 gallons; and
      (3)   Bulk water shall be distributed only through sources designated by
the town. Bulk water from the town’s hydrants may result in potential
contamination, the use of the hydrant will only be allowed with proper safety
equipment and approval from the Mayor and Public Works Director.
   (F)   All monthly bills shall be due and payable by the property owner on or
before the tenth day of the month following that in which the service was
rendered.
   (G)   Property owners shall have the right to dispute the amount of the
water charge until the twentieth day of the month. All monthly bills will
contain a statement informing of the right to dispute the amount of the water
charge by the twentieth day of the month by contacting the Town Clerk in
writing, specifying the factual basis of the dispute. For any such timely
dispute, the Town Clerk, or some other individual designated by the Town Clerk
as an employee empowered to resolve the dispute, shall determine the specific
amount owed, if any. If it is determined the water billing is correct, the town
shall give written notice of such determination together with notification that
unless the billing is paid within five days subsequent, the account will be
deemed delinquent.
   (H)   (1)   Absent a timely dispute, an account will be deemed delinquent if
full payment is not received on or before the tenth day of the month. In such a
case, a service charge equal to 10% of the monthly bill will be assessed on all
delinquent accounts on the twenty-first day of the month.
      (2)   In the event of a timely dispute, a service charge equal to 10% of
the monthly bill will be assessed if the account is deemed delinquent due to
non-payment within five days of the town determining the water billing is
correct.
   (I)   Unless prior payment arrangements have been made with the Town Clerk,
a turn-off fee will be charged if water service is discontinued for any reason,
including delinquency. A turn-on fee, plus all outstanding charges for water
service, service charges, and administrative collection fees incurred in
collecting delinquent accounts, and the turn-off fee must be paid before
service is restored.
   (J)   At any time an account for water service has been delinquent for 60 or
more calendar days, water service may be turned off to the property upon five
calendar days’ notice of the town’s intent to turn off the water service.
Notice of intent to turn off the water service is deemed accomplished if:
      (1)   A notice is deposited in the U.S. Mail with sufficient postage
affixed and addressed to the property owner at the address for billing
purposes; and/or
      (2)   A notice is deposited in the U.S. Mail with sufficient postage
affixed and addressed to the occupant of the premises at the address for
billing purposes.
   (K)   (1)   Turn-on or turn-off by the town: $15;
      (2)   Turn-on or turn-off by any person, firm, or corporation other than
the town: $50; and
      (3)   A work order shall be issued by the Town Clerk upon payment of the
required turn-on or turn-off fee authorizing either the town or a person, firm,
or corporation other than the town to turn on or turn off the water service.
   (L)   (1)   A meter-pull fee of $65 per tap when meter is requested by
property owner to be removed; and
      (2)   A meter-install fee of $65 per tap when meter is requested by
property owner to be installed.
   (M)   Administrative collection fees:
      (1)   Notice of intent to file lien: $15; and
      (2)   File lien statement: $25.
   (N)   A monthly fee for meter maintenance shall be assessed for all
residential, commercial, and industrial properties with meters and lines up to
the following:
 
Meters and Lines Fee
5/8 in.          $10
1 in.            $15
1-1/2 in.        $22.50
2 in.            $30
3 in.            $45.50
4 in.            $60.50
 
   (O)   Monthly water rates for residential, commercial, industrial, and
school users shall increase a minimum of 2.5% per year until a reserve fund of
$250,000 is achieved for infrastructure upgrade expenses.
   (P)   The Town Council may repeal or amend this section at any time it
determines revenue shortfalls have been recovered or at any time it may be in
the best interest of the town.
(Prior Code, § 13.04.030) (Ord. 355, passed 4-9-2013; Ord. 362, passed 7-22-
2014; Ord. 370, passed 5-10-2016; Res. 2018-488, passed 6-20-2018; Res. 2022-
553, passed 5-10-2022; Res. 2022-565, passed 8-9-2022; Ord. 396, passed 4-11-
2023; Res. 2024-606, passed 6-11-2024; Res. 2025-617, passed 6-24-2025)
§ 51.04 DEPOSIT FOR SERVICE.
   The town shall establish an account for each parcel of property to which
water service is provided. All accounts shall be carried in the name of the
property owner, tenant, purchaser, or lessee (collectively referred to as
“occupant”) of each parcel of property. A security payment for each account
established, in the amount of $110, of which $90 will be for the water service
and $20 will be for the sewer service, is required and shall be made payable to
the town. The deposit may be applied to the account at the time for the turning
off of service and to any other amounts, other than criminal fines accessed by
the Municipal Court, owed to the town, regardless of the nature of the
indebtedness.
(Prior Code, § 13.04.040) (Ord. 350, passed 4-8-2012; Ord. 355, passed 4-9-
2013; Ord. 396, passed 4-11-2023)
§ 51.05 RATES AND PAYMENT OF TAP FEES.
   (A)   (1)   The Town Council has the authority to establish by ordinance tap
fees for connecting to the water main. The cost of installing the tap shall be
paid to the town and a work order issued by the Town Clerk prior to making any
connection whatsoever to the water main.
      (2)   The work order shall be issued for a specific person on a specific
property which may be described by street address and shall not be transferable
to other property nor to another person. No water service shall be provided
until the water tap fee is paid in full.
   (B)   After payment of the water tap fee and issuance of a work order, the
town will tap the water main, run the lateral water line from the water main to
the property line, and install a meter and curb stop. All materials and
fixtures required to bring water service to the curb stop, as well as the water
meter, shall be furnished by the town. Thereafter, the property owner shall be
responsible for running and connecting water service from the curb stop into
his or her property.
   (C)   The tap fee is as follows:
      (1)   Five thousand dollars for single-residential or commercial
connections not exceeding three-quarters of one inch in diameter;
      (2)   Five thousand dollars for the first unit plus $50 per unit for each
additional unit for multiple-unit residential or commercial connections not
exceeding three quarters of one inch in diameter;
      (3)   Five thousand five hundred dollars for single-residential or
commercial connections exceeding three-quarters of one inch but not exceeding
one inch in diameter;
      (4)   Five thousand five hundred dollars for the first unit plus $50 per
unit for each additional unit for multiple unit or residential or commercial
connections exceeding three-quarters of one inch but not exceeding one inch in
diameter;
      (5)   Six thousand two hundred and fifty dollars for single-residential
or commercial connections exceeding one inch but not exceeding one and one-half
inch in diameter;
      (6)   Six thousand two hundred and fifty dollars for the first unit plus
$50 per unit for each additional unit for multiple-unit residential or
commercial connections exceeding one inch but not exceeding one and one-half
inch in diameter;
      (7)   Seven thousand and five hundred dollars for single-residential or
commercial connections exceeding one and one-half inch but not exceeding two
inches in diameter;
      (8)   Seven thousand and five hundred dollars for the first unit plus $50
per unit for each additional unit for multiple-unit residential or commercial
connections exceeding one and one-half inch but not exceeding two inches in
diameter; and
      (9)   For any residential or commercial connection exceeding two inches
in diameter, the tap fee shall be negotiated between the applicant and the Town
Council in advance, taking into account conditions existing at the time of the
application, the ability of the waterworks system to furnish the amount of
water required by the applicant, additional administrative expenses that must
be incurred by the town, the costs of labor and materials to complete the tap.
(Prior Code, § 13.04.050) (Ord. 355, passed 4-3-2013; Ord. 386, passed 11-12-
2019; Ord. 406, passed 9-10-2024)
§ 51.06 UNPAID WATER BILLS AND TAP FEES TO BECOME LIEN ON PROPERTY; FORECLOSURE
OF UNPAID BILLS AND FEES.
   (A)   Any account for water services remaining delinquent for 26 calendar
days or more shall be subject to a lien upon the property after the following
steps have been taken.
      (1)   (a)   The town shall give ten calendar days’ notice, in writing, to
the property owner at the address for water billing purposes, of its intent to
file a lien against the property. Such notice shall reasonably describe the
property and set forth the amount claimed by the town. The street address shall
be sufficient to describe the property in the notice.
         (b)   After expiration of the ten-day notice, the town may file a lien
statement, properly acknowledged and containing a true legal description of the
property, in the office of the County Clerk and Official Register of Deeds.
      (2)   Upon the filing of the lien statement, the lien shall be considered
a lien on the property for all purposes.
   (B)   Any such lien may thereafter be foreclosed by appropriate action in a
court of competent jurisdiction or in the manner provided by law for the
foreclosure of a mortgage by advertisement and sale as set forth in the Wyoming
Statutes.
   (C)   The town may, at its option, concurrently pursue turning off water
service to the property and establishing a lien. These remedies are cumulative,
not elective. In addition, the town may, at its option, pursue collection of
the debt in any manner allowed by law at any time after a bill becomes
delinquent.
(Prior Code, § 13.04.060) (Ord. 355, passed 4-13-2013)
§ 51.07 MAINTENANCE OF PRIVATE FACILITIES GENERALLY; FREEZING OF WATER LINES;
OPERATION OF CURB STOPS; INSTALLATION OF PRIVATE WATER LINES.
   (A)   In the event of a broken or frozen consumer line which is located on
town property, the town may make the necessary repairs to that portion of the
consumer line which is located on town property as soon as practicable. The
property owner shall pay all labor and materials reasonably charged by the town
for such repair.
   (B)   In the event of a broken or frozen consumer line on private property,
the property owner shall make the necessary repairs as soon as practicable.
   (C)   If repairs to a broken or frozen consumer line on private property are
not made within a reasonable length of time, considering the elements and
availability of repair equipment, the town may turn off the water service.
   (D)   Only the town, acting through its duly authorized agents and
representatives, can turn off or turn on the valve at the curb stop or at any
other location.
   (E)   All consumer lines shall be buried a minimum of six feet below the
existing grade and shall be backfilled with good material and well tamped.
   (F)   The town shall be responsible to pay any and all costs for material
and labor necessary to maintain and repair municipal water lines, to and
including any curb stop. The property owner, tenant, purchaser, or lessee shall
be responsible to pay any and all costs for material and labor necessary to
maintain and repair water lines from the curb stop to and on his or her
property.
(Prior Code, § 13.04.070) (Ord. 1105-001, passed 1-10-2006) Penalty, see §
51.99
§ 51.08 EXTENSION OF FACILITIES.
   The town will not approve any further plats nor permit any further
development in the unplatted portion of the town or in any property annexed
unless and until the owners of the property shall agree to install main water
lines from the existing town water system, lateral water lines, and curb stops
and meters sufficient to provide water service to the area to be so platted or
developed.
(Prior Code, § 13.04.080)
§ 51.09 WATER CONSERVATION.
   During any time that water is in short supply, or there is any water
emergency whatsoever, the Mayor and the Town Council may establish reasonable
rules and regulations to promote the health, safety and water welfare of the
inhabitants of the town. These reasonable rules and regulations may include,
but not by way of limitation, the power to ration water on any uniform basis
deemed necessary by the Mayor and Town Council.
(Prior Code, § 13.04.090)
§ 51.10 WATER METERS.
   (A)   All such meters are owned and belong to the town.
   (B)   Any time the town has cause to believe a water meter is malfunctioning
in any respect, the town shall repair or replace the meter as soon as
practicable. Such replacement or repairs are at the expense of the town, unless
resulting from neglect, abuse, or tampering by the property owner, tenant,
purchaser, or lessee. The town may turn off water service to any person
refusing to permit the town to repair or replace a meter. In the event of a
dispute regarding malfunction, the town shall have the meter tested for
operation and calibration, with a prevailing party to the dispute to pay all
charges and expenses incurred in testing the operation and calibration of the
meter.
   (C)   From and after the effective date of this section, the town shall
furnish and install the water meters for all new taps, the cost to which is
included it the tap fee established in §
51.05.
   (D)   From and after November 1, 1998, the town will provide water service
only to that property with a radio read meter. Upon the failure to have a radio
read meter, the town may turn off water service until such time as the meter is
installed.
   (E)   All meters shall be maintained by the town and shall be tested and
repaired as necessary. The cost of repairs resulting from neglect, abuse, or
tampering shall be paid by the property owner, tenants, purchaser, or lessee,
and added to and considered a part to the charge for water service.
(Prior Code, § 13.04.100) (Ord. 355, passed 4-9-2013)
§ 51.11 UNLAWFUL ACTS.
   (A)   It is unlawful for any person, firm, or corporation, excepting the
properly authorized agents, officers, or employees of the town, to add, change,
alter, or otherwise modify or tamper with the waterworks system within or
without the town, to insert taps in any water main, or to otherwise connect or
disconnect from any water main.
   (B)   It is unlawful for any person, firm, or corporation, other than the
properly authorized agents, officers, or employees of the town, to turn off or
turn on a valve at the curb stop or at any other location unless that person,
firm, or corporation has first paid the fee and received a work order from the
town to do so.
   (C)   It shall be unlawful for any person to tamper or interfere with any
meter, or meter seal or remote, or to so arrange the water service or piping so
that the use of water will not actuate the meter.
(Prior Code, § 13.04.101) Penalty, see §
51.99
§ 51.12 ESTIMATION OF WATER USAGE POLICY.
   Because of the loss of revenue when estimating the water usage at the
minimum, the Accounting Office can look back to the previous year during the
same time period that is in question to get a water usage history.
(Res. 2012-415, passed 11-13-2012)
§ 51.13 WATER CONNECTION REQUIRED.
   (A)   It is unlawful for any person to inhabit or reside in any structure
within the corporate limits of the town unless the structure is connected with
the town water system in accordance with the provisions of this subchapter.
   (B)   Any person inhabiting or residing in any structure within the town
that is disconnected from the town’s water system for non-payment of water fees
shall be in violation of this section and subject to penalties set forth in §
51.99.
(Ord. 412, passed 9-9-2025) Penalty, see §
51.99
**** EMERGENCY WATER RATIONING ****
§ 51.25 DEFINITION.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definition applies unless
the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   USER.
      (1)   Any user, residential, commercial, or industrial, within the town
limits;
      (2)   Any user, residential, commercial, or industrial, outside of the
town limits, but who receives or purchases water from the town’s water supply;
and/or
      (3)   Any agency or form of government agency which uses water for any
purpose other than drinking or for sanitary functions: schools, post offices,
and the like.
(Prior Code, § 8.16.010) (Ord. 56, passed - -1977)
§ 51.26 WATERING SCHEDULE.
   (A)   Any user whose house number or number assigned by the town to any
business, residence, or vacant lot, with lawn, grass, or garden planted
therein, whose number ends with an even number will be allowed to water on
Mondays, Wednesdays, and Fridays between the hours of 7:00 p.m. and 9:00 p.m.
   (B)   Any user whose house number or number assigned by the town to any
business, residence, or vacant lot, with lawn, grass, or garden planted
therein, whose number ends with an odd number will be allowed to water on
Tuesdays, Thursdays, and Saturdays between the hours of 7:00 p.m. and 9:00 p.m.
   (C)   Hanna Elk Mountain School will be allowed to water the football field
for two hours between the hours of 8:00 p.m. and 10:00 p.m. on Mondays,
Tuesdays, Wednesdays, and Thursdays.
   (D)   Any user outside of the town limits must submit to the town office,
within seven days of the ordinance codified in this subchapter being published,
their watering schedule, not to be more than six hours per week.
(Prior Code, § 8.16.020) (Ord. 56, passed - -1977) Penalty, see §
51.99
§ 51.27 WASTE OF WATER PROHIBITED.
   No person shall permit any waste of water through excessive sprinkling, or
sprinkling out of prescribed hours, faulty fixtures, leaking hose, or any cause
whatever. If any person permits any waste of water, the Commissioner shall
forthwith cause the water to be turned off from the premises upon which such
waste shall occur, until the cause of the waste is corrected and all charges
and fines for the violation of this section, including the fee of $15 for
reconnecting such premises with the municipal waterworks system, are paid.
(Prior Code, § 8.16.030) (Ord. 56, passed - -1977) Penalty, see §
51.99
§ 51.28 ABATEMENT OF RATES TO INDIGENT PERSONS.
   The Town Council shall have authority, in its discretion, to grant abatement
upon the acts of this subchapter to indigent persons.
(Prior Code, § 8.16.040) (Ord. 56, passed - -1977)
§ 51.29 GARDEN VARIANCES.
   Any person who is living within the town limits on a fixed income may apply
to the Town Council for a variance for one hour every other day of the week
(excluding Saturday and Sunday) for watering their garden (and garden only).
GARDEN means any plot of ground where herbs, fruits, and vegetables are
cultivated.
(Prior Code, § 8.16.050) (Ord. 56, passed - -1977)
§ 51.30 ENFORCEMENT.
   Enforcement of this chapter will be by any police officer, the Water
Commissioner, or any employee of the town. Enforcement may be honored and
enforced by filing a written complaint with the town’s Police Justice.
(Prior Code, § 8.16.060) (Ord. 56, passed - -1977)
§ 51.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person who violates any provision of §§
51.01 through
51.12 shall, upon conviction, be guilty of a misdemeanor and subject to the
fine as set forth in the general penalty provisions of the town. Each day of
violation or any portion thereof shall constitute a separate offense.
(Prior Code, § 13.04.110)
   (C)   Any person who violates any of the provisions of §§
51.25 through
51.30 or any part of the rules or regulations of the Water Commission approved
by the Town Council or who fails to pay any assessment, charge, or rent as
provided by §§
51.25 through
51.30, or fails to do any act or thing required of him or her by the provisions
of §§
51.25 through
51.30, shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon conviction thereof,
before the Municipal (Police) Justice of the town, he or she shall be fined not
less than $20, nor more than $200 for each offense.
(Prior Code, § 8.16.070)
(Ord. 56, passed - -1977; Ord. 270, passed - -1998)
