**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 90.01 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions apply unless
the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   FEED. All provisions of edible or drinkable material, including, without
limitation:
      (1)   Bones;
      (2)   Salt licks;
      (3)   Water;
      (4)   Hay, grain, or grain mixtures; and
      (5)   Feed blocks.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.084)
   FOWL. Live chickens, laying hens, turkeys, dugs, geese, or carrier pigeons.
   HOUSEHOLD PET. Any fully domesticated animal kept for personal
companionship, including, but not limited to, a dog, cat, reptile, bird, or
rodent, except that any animal defined as livestock, fowl, or as a wild,
exotic, or dangerous animal cannot be considered a HOUSEHOLD PET.
   LIVESTOCK. Animals kept for farm purposes, especially marketable animals
sought after for their milk or meat, such as cattle, horses, turkeys, geese,
pigs, rabbits, goats, llamas, alpacas, and sheep, except that any animal
defined as fowl or a wild, exotic, or dangerous animal cannot be considered
LIVESTOCK.
   WILD, EXOTIC, OR DANGEROUS ANIMAL. Any animal not considered a domestic pet,
fowl, or livestock and barnyard animal, as those terms are defined in this
section, including, but not limited to:
      (1)   Bears;
      (2)   Any species of feline other than ordinary domesticated house cats;
      (3)   Skunks;
      (4)   Poisonous reptiles;
      (5)   Raccoons;
      (6)   Deer, moose, antelope, or elk;
      (7)   Any species of non-human primate;
      (8)   Alligators, caimans, crocodiles, or other species of the order
Crocodilia;
      (9)   Monitor lizards and tegus;
      (10)   Nonpoisonous snakes with a length greater than six feet;
      (11)   Poisonous spiders;
      (12)   Scorpions;
      (13)   Any animal species deemed to be invasive by either the State
Department of Agriculture, the State Department of Game and Fish, or the County
Weed and Pest District; and
      (14)   Any animal species the keeping of which is prohibited by the laws
of the state or of the United States of America.
(Ord. 266, passed - -1998; Ord. 407, passed 10-8-2024)
§ 90.02 RUNNING AT LARGE PROHIBITED.
   No cattle, horses, hogs, sheep, or goats, or chickens, ducks, geese,
turkeys, guinea fowls, or any other fowl or animal, except dogs as provided in
this chapter, and cats shall at any time be permitted to run at large within
the town.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.010) (Ord. 81, passed - -1979)
§ 90.03 ESTABLISHMENT OF ANIMAL POUND.
   (A)   An animal pound will be made available by the town. The pound shall be
in the charge of the Pound Master or Town Marshal.
   (B)   The Town Marshal or any other person so designated is empowered to act
as Pound Master. He or she shall receive no pay other than his or her regular
pay as Town Marshal.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.020) (Ord. 81, passed - -1979)
§ 90.04 IMPOUNDMENT; ENFORCEMENT; REDEMPTION; FEE.
   (A)   If any animal or fowl referred to in §
90.02 is found running at large, it shall be the duty of the Pound Master or
Town Marshal to impound such animal in a secure pen, pound, or other place
provided for that purpose.
   (B)   No animal so impounded shall be released until the owner shall have
paid a fee determined by the governing body to be fair and just for the
impoundment and care of the type of animal so impounded.
   (C)   (1)   Immediately upon impounding any animal, the Pound Master or Town
Marshal, or other agents appointed by the town, shall record in a book provided
by the town a description of the animal.
      (2)   The record shall state the sex, color, breed, estimated age,
distinguishing marks, the date and hour of impounding, and the final
disposition of the animal.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.030) (Ord. 81, passed - -1979)
§ 90.05 SALE OF UNREDEEMED ANIMALS.
   (A)   (1)   It shall be the duty of the Town Marshal to sell any animal or
fowl so impounded, upon giving at least five days’ previous notice to the owner
or person in charge of the impounded animal of the time and place of such sale.
      (2)   The notice shall describe the animal with reasonable accuracy;
provided, that any such animal may be redeemed at any time before the sale by
payment of the fees and charges provided for in §
90.04.
   (B)   (1)   In case any animal or fowl, sold pursuant to the provisions of
this section, shall be sold for more than an amount sufficient to pay the fees
and charges required pursuant to §
90.04, the excess shall be deposited with the Town Treasurer.
      (2)   Such excess shall be paid to the owner of such animal or to the
person entitled to the possession of such animal, upon the claim and proper
proof presented by such person.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.040) (Ord. 81, passed - -1979)
§ 90.06 BREAKING OPEN POUND PROHIBITED.
   No person shall, directly or indirectly, break open, or assist in breaking
open, any pen or enclosure with the intention of releasing any animal confined
therein pursuant to the provisions of this chapter.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.050) (Ord. 81, passed - -1979) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.07 CRUELTY TO ANIMALS.
   No person shall overdrive, overload, drive when overloaded, overwork, or
willfully and maliciously torture, torment, deprive of necessary sustenance,
unnecessarily or cruelly beat, or willfully and maliciously and needlessly
mutilate or kill, or carry in or upon any vehicle, or otherwise mistreat in a
cruel or inhumane manner, any animal, or cause or procure it to be done, or
having the charge and custody of any animal, unnecessarily fail to provide it
with the proper food, drink, or protection from the weather, or cruelly abandon
it.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.060) (Ord. 81, passed - -1979) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.08 REMOVAL AND DISPOSAL OF DEAD ANIMALS.
   It shall be the duty of the owner of any animal found dead within the town
to remove such animal beyond the corporate limits of the town and to bury such
animal; provided, that if the town has designated a particular area for the
burial of dead animals, the owner of any dead animal shall remove the dead
animal to such designated area and bury the dead animal therein at the place
designated by the administrative official in charge of such designated burial
area.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.070) (Ord. 81, passed - -1979) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.09 OWNERSHIP, KEEPING, OR FEEDING PROHIBITED.
   No person shall own, keep, or feed any wild, exotic, or dangerous animal,
including without limitation:
   (A)   Bears;
   (B)   Any species of feline other than ordinary domesticated house cats;
   (C)   Skunks;
   (D)   Poisonous reptiles;
   (E)   Raccoons;
   (F)   Deer, moose, antelope, or elk;
   (G)   Any species of non-human primate, but excluding animals imported under
authority of state or federal law.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.080) (Ord. 266, passed - -1998; Ord. 407, passed 10-8-2024)
Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.10 LICENSE REQUIRED FOR THOSE WILD AND EXOTIC ANIMALS NOT OTHERWISE
SPECIFIED.
   No person shall own, keep, or feed any other wild or domestic animal, which
has not been specifically prohibited in §
90.09, within the town for which a state license is required, unless such
person possesses the appropriate license from the State Game and Fish.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.082) (Ord. 266, passed - -1998) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.11 DEFENSE.
   It is a specific defense to a charge of violating §
90.09 that a person is feeding only squirrels and birds.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.086) (Ord. 266, passed - -1998)
§ 90.12 KEEPING LIVESTOCK WITHIN TOWN LIMITS PROHIBITED; EXCEPTION.
   It shall be unlawful for any person or persons, partnership, corporation, or
association to keep and maintain within the corporate limits of the town any
livestock, subject to the following exceptions.
   (A)   In case of emergency, the Mayor may give permission to keep saddle
horses within the corporate limits of the town for no more than 24 hours.
   (B)   Livestock may be kept for education purposes, subject to the zoning
requirements of this code.
(Prior Code, § 6.12.010) (Ord. 407, passed 10-8-2024) Penalty, see §
90.99
**** DOGS ****
§ 90.25 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions apply unless
the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   KENNEL. Any premises or property of any kind or description where more than
one female dog is kept for breeding purposes or where more than one litter of
puppies or dogs is kept for purposes of sale.
   OWNER. Any person keeping or harboring a dog.
   POUND. Any place provided for the impounding of animals.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.010) (Ord. 81, passed - -1979)
§ 90.26 REGISTRATION AND LICENSING; DUTIES OF TOWN CLERK.
   It is the duty of the Town Clerk to annually make a complete enumeration and
registration of all dogs within the town by publication at least once in a
newspaper of general circulation in the town and to serve notice on the owners
or keepers of dogs to register them. He or she shall collect the fees and issue
receipt for all registrations provided in this subchapter, and it shall be his
or her duty to make remittance of all amounts so collected to the Town
Treasurer.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.020) (Ord. 81, passed - -1979)
§ 90.27 REGISTRATION AND LICENSING; REQUIRED; FEES; EXPIRATION.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful for any person to keep or harbor any dog over six
months of age in the town unless he or she shall cause the same to be
registered and licensed by the Town Clerk and shall pay to the Town Clerk a
registration fee as provided below:
      (1)   Five dollars for each neutered male or spayed female dog; and
      (2)   Ten dollars for each un-neutered male or un-spayed female dog.
   (B)   All registration for dogs as provided in this chapter shall expire
December 31 and be due on January 1 of each year. Registration must be
completed within 30 days thereafter. All dogs brought into the town on or after
July 1 of any year shall be licensed at one-half the regular fee.
   (C)   All dogs newly brought into town shall be registered within 30 days.
   (D)   All young dogs shall be registered within 30 days after becoming six
months old.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.030) (Ord. 81, passed - -1979; Ord. 141, passed - -1982;
Ord. 1005-001, passed 1-10-2006) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.28 VACCINATION CERTIFICATE PREREQUISITE TO LICENSE ISSUANCE.
   No license shall be issued under this subchapter unless the applicant has
obtained and exhibited to the Town Clerk a certificate or tag issued by a
licensed veterinarian showing that the dog sought to be licensed has, within
one year last past and immediately preceding the date of such application for a
license, been vaccinated for rabies.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.040) (Ord. 81, passed - -1979)
§ 90.29 REGISTRATION RECEIPT AND BOOK; ISSUANCE AND WEARING OF TAG.
   (A)   Upon collection of each registration fee, the Town Clerk shall issue a
receipt therefor and register the animal for which such fee is paid in a book
kept for that purpose, which book shall describe each animal by tag number,
sex, name, color, age, kind of dog, and name and address of the owner or
keeper, and if a female, whether spayed or not. He or she shall give to the
owner or keeper a license tag bearing the number under which such dog is
registered and the year of registration.
   (B)   Such registration tag shall be secured by the owner around the neck of
the animal registered, and it shall be unlawful for any person to put upon any
dog any tag or check of the kind required by this subchapter, except the one
delivered by the Town Clerk for that particular animal.
   (C)   If the dog tags are lost or destroyed, a duplicate tag shall be issued
by the Town Clerk upon presentation of the receipt showing payment for the
license for the current year and payment of $1 for the duplicate tag.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.050) (Ord. 81, passed - -1979) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.30 IMPOUNDMENT; REQUIRED WHEN.
   (A)   Any dog found running at large within the town is declared to be a
public nuisance; it is the duty of the town police or Pound Master to pick up
and confine any such dog.
   (B)   In the event the Town Marshal or Pound Master knows or is able to
locate the owner of any dog found running at large within the town without a
current and valid license or tag as required by this subchapter, he or she may
return the dog to the owner with a citation for violation of this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.060) (Ord. 1005-001, passed 1-10-2006) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.31 IMPOUNDMENT; REDEMPTION BY OWNER.
   The owner or agent of any impounded dog shall have the privilege of claiming
such dog within five days after it is impounded, upon payment of an impoundment
or boarding fee of $20 a day as established by resolution of the Town Council
for each day that the dog is impounded or boarded. No care fee will be imposed
for the day it is impounded. The impoundment or boarding fee is in addition to
securing a registration for such dog for the current year, in accordance with
the provision of this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.070) (Ord. 81, passed - -1979; Ord. 102, passed - -1980;
Ord. 223, passed - -1989; Ord. 319, passed - -2008; Res. 2008-320, passed 10-
14-2008)
§ 90.32 IMPOUNDMENT; REDEMPTION BY PERSON OTHER THAN OWNER; DISPOSITION OF
UNCLAIMED DOGS.
   (A)   Any impounded dog not claimed by the owner within five days may be
redeemed by any person who secures a license for such dog as provided in this
subchapter.
   (B)   Dogs not redeemed by anyone shall be disposed of by the Town Marshal
or Pound Master in the most humane manner available at the time of such
disposal. No dog wearing a registration tag shall be destroyed unless three
days’ previous notice, by certified mail, is given to the owner or person in
charge of the dog. The Pound Master or Town Marshal may, in preference to
putting the dog to death, turn the dog over to some person who has agreed to
and does permanently remove it from the town. Payment of the license and
impounding fees are not required when the dog is permanently removed from the
town.
   (C)   The Town Marshal may charge a fee as established by resolution of the
Town Council for the euthanization of an unclaimed dog or the nonredemption of
a dog from the animal pound by an owner of the dog. The charge for the
euthanization is $150.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.080) (Ord. 81, passed - -1979; Ord. 102, passed - -1980;
Ord. 319, passed - -2008; Res. 2008-320, passed 10-14-2008)
§ 90.33 PROCEDURE FOR COLLECTION OF FEES FROM THE ANIMAL SHELTER.
   (A)   Effective immediately, the following procedure will be followed in
reference to §§
90.31 and
90.32. When a citizen is requesting boarding of animals such as dogs, a
boarding fee will be presented to him or her. The Town Marshal’s office will
advise the citizen of the amount due prior to release of their animals. Payment
by the citizen can be made either to Town Hall or the Marshal’s office.
   (B)   If the citizen is unable to make full payment of these fees due to
special circumstances, the Marshal, the Marshal’s designee, Town Clerk, or Town
Treasurer may make payment arrangements when necessary. The Treasurer’s office
will be notified with any payment arrangements. When possible, the Marshal’s
office will not hold a citizen’s animal(s) in lieu of full payment at the time
of pick up as long as payment arrangements are made.
   (C)   If a citizen is requesting boarding for longer than five days, the
town may request half of the boarding fee upfront to cover costs of the animal
shelter such as food and other related expenditures.
   (D)   If a boarded dog is not reclaimed by the owner within 24 hours of
actual notification, the owner will be notified by certified mail that the dog
(s) will remain at the shelter for a period of five days at the expense of the
owner, after which time the dog(s) will be adopted out if possible and, as a
last resort, humanely destroyed.
   (E)   The Town Marshal may charge a fee, as established by resolution of the
Town Council, for the euthanization of an unclaimed or the non-redemption of a
dog at the animal pound by an owner of the dog.
   (F)   The HMO Clerk is to keep a record of when animals are boarded and due
for pickup as an internal control for the amount of moneys to be collected.
(Res. 2012-416, passed 11-13-2012)
§ 90.34 VICIOUS DOGS.
   No person shall own, keep, or harbor or allow to be upon any premises
occupied by him or her or in or under his or her charge or control any
notoriously vicious dog that may manifest a disposition to any person or
domestic animal, without having the dog properly secured and or muzzled. Upon
conviction of a violation of this section, if it appears to the court that the
dog is still living, the court may, in addition to punishing the person
convicted, order the animal control officer to forthwith cause such dog to be
humanely destroyed, and for that purpose, any such officer charged with such
duty shall have the right to enter upon any premises within the town.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.090) (Ord. 81, passed - -1979) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.35 DOGS RUNNING AT LARGE.
   It is unlawful for any owner or keeper of any dog to permit or suffer such
dog to run at large within the town. Every such dog found running at large
within the town shall be deemed a nuisance and the owner shall, on conviction,
be deemed guilty of maintaining a nuisance. Whenever any dog nuisance comes to
the notice of the Pound Master or Town Marshal, it shall be his or her duty to
immediately abate the nuisance by securing and confining the dog, or causing
the owner to do so. If the owner is not known or cannot be found and it is
impracticable for the Pound Master or Town Marshal to properly secure and
confine the dog, the dog may be killed. If necessary, the pound master or Town
Marshal may follow the dog outside the town limits.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.100) (Ord. 81, passed - -1979) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.36 BARKING DOGS.
   (A)   No person shall keep, harbor, or allow to stay about any premises
occupied or controlled by him or her any dog to cause annoyance, alarm, or
noise disturbance at any time of the day or night, by repeated barking,
whining, screeching, howling, braying, or other like sounds which can be heard
by either an animal control officer or an individual residing within 300 feet
(exclusive of public right-of-way) of the place where the dog is harbored or to
refuse or intentionally fail to cease the unreasonable noise when ordered to do
so by the town’s code enforcement officer or law enforcement officer. Such a
dog shall be deemed a public nuisance.
   (B)   Prima facie evidence of a violation of this section shall include, but
not be limited to:
      (1)   Vocalizing (howling, yelping, barking, squawking, and the like) by
the dog for five minutes WITHOUT INTERRUPTION, defined as an average of four or
more vocalizations per minute in that period; or
      (2)   Vocalizing by the dog for 20 minutes INTERMITTENTLY, defined as an
average of two vocalizations or more per minute in that period.
   (C)   It is an affirmative defense under this section that the dog or other
animal was intentionally provoked to bark or make any other noise.
   (D)    Before any prosecution is brought, it shall be necessary for the
complaining party or a law enforcement officer of the town to notify the owner
or other persons keeping such dog that the dog is creating a disturbance, and
that unless such nuisance is abated after receipt of such notice, a complaint
may be filed. Such notification, if by the complaining party, shall be by
registered or certified mail, return receipt requested, and shall be deemed
complete upon deposit of such notice in the United States Mail, postage
prepaid, properly addressed. If notice is by a law enforcement officer, it
shall be complete upon personal notification by the officer. At the same time
of notice, a copy of the notice shall be provided to the Animal Control Officer
or Chief of Police.
   (E)   Any such animal shall be deemed a nuisance and may be seized and
impounded by the town law enforcement officer if the disturbance reoccurs after
the owner of such animal has received three documented offenses from the town.
If any person refuses to let such officer or employee enter the place where the
animal is located, or refuses to let such officer or employee take possession
of the animal, such town officer or employee shall obtain a court order
permitting entering the premises and taking such animal.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.110) (Ord. 340, passed 8-10-2010) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.37 LOOSING OF STRAYS.
   No person shall dump or in any manner turn loose any dog to stray upon the
streets of the town.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.120) (Ord. 81, passed - -1979) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.38 KENNELS.
   (A)   Definitions. For the purpose of this section, the following
definitions apply unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different
meaning.
      COMMERCIAL KENNEL. A business which offers breeding, buying, selling,
boarding, or training of dogs or puppies, regardless of the number of animals
maintained.
      HOBBY KENNEL. A residence or other property where more than four dogs are
maintained, except in cases when the total number of dogs includes puppies of
the same litter plus two additional dogs.
      WRITTEN NOTICE. Notice, in writing, delivered by hand to an individual
over 18 years of age on the kennel property or affixed to a conspicuous part of
the kennel property.
   (B)   Zoning.
      (1)   Commercial kennels are allowed in the town only in appropriately
zoned areas.
      (2)   Hobby kennels are allowed in all areas of town.
   (C)   Licensing.
      (1)   A license for a commercial kennel or a hobby kennel shall require:
         (a)   An application, listing the:
            1.   Number of dogs;
            2.   Breed of each dog;
            3.   Sex of each dog;
            4.   Age of each dog;
            5.   Weight of each dog; and
            6.   Rabies tag information for each dog.
         (b)   Payment of the investigation fee as defined by this code;
         (c)   An investigation by an Animal Control Officer or member of the
Marshal’s office to ensure compliance with this code;
         (d)   Payment of the required license fee as defined by this code;
         (e)   Proof of notification per letter form of all neighboring
properties within a 750-foot radius; and
         (f)   Approval of 65% of neighboring properties within the 750-foot
radius of location issued.
      (2)   Each license shall be applied for and renewed annually from the
date the license is issued.
   (D)   Fees.
      (1)   For a commercial kennel, the license fee is $500.
      (2)   For a hobby kennel, the license fee is $300.
      (3)   The investigation fee is $50, due annually at renewal of license.
   (E)   Kennel requirements. Each kennel shall meet the following
requirements.
      (1)   Shelter.
         (a)   Each animal shall have access to an area sheltered from adverse
weather conditions.
         (b)   Each animal shall be able to comfortably walk, turn, and lie
down in the shelter.
         (c)   Enough shelter access shall be provided so that each dog can
utilize the shelter simultaneously.
      (2)   Water. Each dog shall have access to drinking water at all times.
      (3)   Size.
         (a)   Small breeds, under ten pounds:
            1.   Six feet in width by ten feet in depth for four dogs; and
            2.   Additional two feet in width for each additional dog.
         (b)   Medium dogs, ten to 100 pounds:
            1.   Fourteen feet in width by 12 feet in length for four dogs; and
            2.   Add four feet in width for each additional dog.
         (c)   Large breeds, over 100 pounds:
            1.   Eighteen feet in width by 20 feet in length for four dogs; and
            2.   Add six feet in width for each additional dog.
         (d)   For facilities including breeds of multiple sizes, the largest
animal will be determinative for space purposes.
      (4)   Cleanliness. Each kennel shall be kept free of feces.
      (5)   Safety. Each kennel shall be free of jagged or sharp edges that
could harm any dog residing within.
      (6)   Perimeter fence.
         (a)   Each kennel shall have a perimeter fence of at least six feet in
height.
         (b)   Each perimeter fence shall be of material that prevents other
animals from entering the kennel.
      (7)   List of animals maintained.
         (a)   Each owner shall maintain a current list of animals within the
kennel, detailing the information required under division (C)(1)(a) above.
         (b)   Each owner shall provide the town, through the Clerk’s office,
notice within 30 days of any change to the information on the list of animals
maintained.
   (F)   Inspection of kennel. With three days’ written notice to the owner, an
animal control officer or member of the Town Marshal’s office shall be allowed
to inspect the premises to ensure compliance with this chapter.
   (G)   Revocation of kennel license.
      (1)   A kennel license shall be revoked for the following reasons:
         (a)   Refusal of permission for an inspection;
         (b)   Failing to abide by the terms of this section; or
         (c)   The applicant is convicted of more than two violations of this
chapter in a two-year period.
      (2)   Prior to the revocation of a kennel license, written notice shall
be given to the owner.
      (3)   If able, the owner may correct any defect resulting in license
renewal within seven days. Proof of any correction shall be at the inspection
of an animal control officer or a member of the Town Marshal’s office.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.130) (Ord. 384, passed 8-13-2019) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.39 DUTIES OF ANIMAL CONTROL OFFICER WHEN ANIMAL BITES.
   (A)   It shall be the duty of the animal control officer, when a complaint
is received that a dog has bitten a person, to take the dog into custody and
notify the owner, if known, and the Town-County Health Department that the dog
will be impounded for a period of ten calendar days; provided, however, that at
the owner’s written request the animal control officer shall release the dog
into the custody of a licensed veterinarian who shall impound and keep the dog
for a period of ten calendar days at the owner’s expense.
   (B)   If, after the end of the ten-day period, such dog shall exhibit no
symptoms of rabies, the dog shall be returned to the owner by the veterinarian,
or if impounded in the town animal center, it shall be returned to the owner by
the animal control officer upon payment of all accrued charges. If the owner
has not been found, the dog shall be disposed of after the ten-day period. In
either case, the Town-County Health Department will be notified after the
elapse of the ten-day impoundment period. (Prior Code, § 6.08.140) (Ord. 81,
passed - -1979; Ord. 132, passed - -1981)
§ 90.40 DUTIES OF POLICE AND ANIMAL CONTROL AGENCY WHEN BITING ANIMAL CANNOT BE
CAPTURED.
   No police officer or other person shall kill, or cause to be killed, any
animal suspected of being rabid, except after the animal has been placed in
quarantine, and notification has been made to the Town-County Health
Department, and the diagnosis of rabies made by a licensed veterinarian. If a
veterinarian diagnoses rabies in an animal in quarantine, then the animal shall
be humanely killed and the head of such animal sent to a laboratory for
pathological examination and confirmation of the diagnosis. The only exception
to this shall be if the animal in question is so vicious and/or wild that it
cannot be restrained and the police officer or animal control officer feels
that further pursuit would result in losing the animal, then the animal can be
killed with gunshot not aimed at the head of the animal. After the animal is
dead, the head is to be severed from the body, and the head sent to the State
Laboratory for examination.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.150) (Ord. 81, passed - -1979; Ord. 132, passed - -1981)
§ 90.41 FEMALE DOGS IN HEAT TO BE CONFINED.
   Every female dog in heat shall be confined in a building or secure enclosure
in such a manner that such female dog cannot contact another animal except for
planned breeding.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.151) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.42 NUISANCES AND DESTRUCTIVE DOGS.
   No person shall fail to exercise proper care and control to prevent any dog
owned by him or her or in or under his or her charge from excessive,
continuous, or untimely barking, molesting passersby, chasing vehicles or
bicycles, attacking other domestic animals, trespassing upon school grounds, or
trespassing upon or damaging private property. Upon conviction of a violation
of this section involving damage or injury to persons, property, or other
domestic animals, if it appears to the court that the dog is still living, the
court may, in addition to punishing the person convicted, order the animal
control officer to forthwith cause such dog to be humanely destroyed, and for
that purpose, any such officer charged with such duty shall have the right to
enter upon any premises within the town.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.152) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.43 DOG FECES AND PUBLIC HEALTH.
   It shall be the responsibility of the owner to keep his or her property free
and clear of dog feces so as not to present potential danger to the public
health as determined by the County Health Officer. In addition, the owner or
person in control of any dog shall be responsible for picking up any feces on
public or private property when walking the animal.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.153) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.44 MAIMING OR POISONING DOGS PROHIBITED.
   No person shall willfully kill, maim, or disfigure any dog, or administer
poison or cause to be ingested any foreign object to any dog or expose any
poisonous substance with the intent that it shall be taken by any dog except
pests of public health importance. The provisions of this section do not in any
way limit the right of a police officer to kill any dog if said officer
determines that there is a reasonable danger to the public safety or persons to
defend themselves or their property.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.154) Penalty, see §
90.99
**** FOWL ****
§ 90.55 KEEPING CERTAIN ANIMALS WITHIN TOWN LIMITS.
   Except as specifically provided by zoning classification pursuant to
Chapter 153, no person shall keep or cause to be kept any fowl unless same are
permitted pursuant to the provisions of§
90.56 of this chapter.
(Prior Code, § 6.10.020) (Ord. 349, passed 7-22-2014) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.56 CONDITIONAL USE PERMITS FOR FOWL.
   (A)   Those persons wishing to keep fowl on their property within the town
must apply for a permit.
   (B)   The granting of the permit set forth in division (A) above shall be in
addition to all other state and federal permits, statutes, and regulations
regarding the care and keeping of fowl for which a permit is sought.
   (C)   An application for a permit to board or keep fowl within the town
shall be made to the Town Clerk and shall contain the following:
      (1)   Name and address of applicant;
      (2)   Location and legal description; and
      (3)   Plans and specifications addressing the restrictions noted herein.
   (D)   When, upon review of the application, the Town Clerk is satisfied that
the proposed plan meets the requirements and regulations of this section, and
the code of the town, and provided approval has been received from the Town
Planning Commission and/or Town Council, a permit shall be issued.
   (E)   Upon the issuance of a permit for fowl (chickens, laying hens, and the
like), the Town Clerk may place such conditions and restrictions upon the
permit as it deems reasonable and necessary, including, but not limited to:
      (1)   No permittee/family unit shall have more than six fowl (chickens,
laying hens, and the like);
      (2)   Male fowl (chickens, laying hens, and the like) are prohibited;
      (3)   Fowl (chickens, laying hens, and the like) must be kept in a coop
in a rear yard and must remain in the coop or an adjoining fenced enclosure at
all times;
      (4)   Enclosures which are subject to zoning standards for fence height
and accessory buildings but be at least seven and one-half feet from any
property line and 40 feet from any neighboring houses;
      (5)   The maximum size of a coop or enclosure may not exceed ten square
feet per chicken, and both together may not exceed 15 square feet per chicken;
      (6)   Fencing, landscape buffering, or screening of a minimum of six feet
in height and enclosing the entire perimeter of the coop and chicken exercise
area (yard) must be installed and maintained at all times;
      (7)   There shall be no slaughtering of fowl (chickens, laying hens, and
the like) on the premises. Harvesting of the chicken for meat or any purpose
other than eggs shall be conducted at a USDA-approved facility;
      (8)   Provisions for removal of excrement from the property on a regular
basis; and
      (9)   Any other matter which the Town Council deems reasonably pertinent
or relevant to the issuance of the permit.
   (F)   Any person whose application for a license under this subchapter has
been denied may request and shall be granted a hearing on the matter before the
Town Council.
   (G)   Whenever, upon inspection, it is found that conditions or practices
exist which are in violation of any provisions of this section or regulations
issued under this section, the town shall give notice in writing to the person
to whom the permit was issued that unless such conditions or practices are
corrected within a reasonable period of time specified in the notice by the
town, the permit will be suspended. At the end of such period, the town shall
re-inspect such conditions or practices have not been corrected, the Town Clerk
shall suspend the permit and give notice in writing of such suspension to the
person to whom the license is issued. Upon receipt of notice of suspension,
such person shall cease keeping fowl.
   (H)   Any person whose license has been suspended, or who has received
notice from the town that his or her permit will be suspended unless certain
conditions are corrected, may request and shall be granted a hearing on the
matter before the Town Council; provided, that when no petition for such
hearing has been filed within ten days following the day on which notice of
suspension was served, such license shall be deemed to have been automatically
revoked at the expiration of such ten-day period.
(Prior Code, § 6.10.030) (Ord. 349, passed 7-22-2014) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person or persons, partnership, corporation, or association
violating any of the provisions of §
90.12 shall, upon conviction, be fined in the sum of not less than $10 nor more
than $100 for each violation.
(Prior Code, § 6.12.020)
   (C)   The fine for having a dog running at large within the town, in
violation of §
90.30, is as follows:
      (1)   For the first offense: $50;
      (2)   For the second offense: $75;
      (3)   For the third offense: $100; and
      (4)   The fourth offense is a misdemeanor with mandatory court appearance
and fine at the discretion of the Municipal Judge.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.060)
   (D)   Any person violating any provision of §§
90.25 through
90.44 shall be punished in accordance with §
10.99.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.155)
(Ord. 21, passed - -1971; Ord. 1005-001, passed 1- -2006; Ord. 1005-001, passed
1-10-2006)
